# Results: learned timeouts against the oracle on 5 machines

Updated 2026-11-01 13:19; FREEZE restart-v7 (30dcd0ed8351), each section naming the frozen code its rows ran under. Each configuration has one or more draws (full-spec runs, four replicas and the client on five CloudLab machines; results/<protocol>/<run>_<draw>/). Per protocol, three summaries give each configuration's R, throughput and latency (whole run and inner) as the mean over its draws and each draw's value, and the tables after them give every draw as its own row. Each run is scored by the client judge: R = tps / N minus the client's latency in seconds (the commit-weighted mean of its per-second p50, a chunk with no commits at 10 s) per 20 s chunk, averaged per phase (the first 20 s after each onset skipped; the warm-up before the first onset and anything after the last phase's 300 s, the run's planned end, are outside every window), N the protocol's offered rate. Mean R is over the spec's phases; inner R (and the inner throughput and latency) leave out the first and the last phase, the base phases that open and close every spec (Kevin, 2026-10-21). z = seconds without a commit in the phase. Rows are sorted by their configuration's mean R over its draws, then by draw; the set (A, B or C) a draw ran on is in brackets (every set pulls rows from one queue per protocol). Pretrained = the simulator prior installed (its sim-gate verdict in brackets); every model learns online from the run's own rewards. Floor = the protocol's latency floor (multiplier x a hold-free signal + offset). Every engine and model opens at the ladder's middle rung. Generated by results/results_md.py from the runs in results/<protocol>/.
## PBFT

N 20,000; ladder 250/550/1200, opening 550; heuristic k 3.96 on pre_prepare_latency_ms+prepare_latency_ms+commit_latency_ms; floor 1.5 x pre_prepare_latency_ms+prepare_latency_ms+commit_latency_ms + 100 ms; range 100-3000 ms.

Phases: p0 base, p1 base + hold D300, p2 base + hold D550, p3 5+50, p4 5+50 + hold D500, p5 5+150, p6 base.

Rows under restart-v4 (2026-10-22). The earlier restart-v3 rows were removed (not kept in this repository): that freeze dated the request timer from the proposal tick that admits a request rather than from its receipt, so T 250 never expired at 5+50 and the ladder did not separate (DESIGN.md section 2). Recalibrated on restart-cal8.

Rows ran under frozen code restart-v4 (a424745337c6), restart-v7 (30dcd0ed8351).

## SBFT

N 20,000; ladder 250/650/1600 x 10/150, opening 650/10; heuristic k 4.2 on pre_prepare_latency_ms+prepare_latency_ms+commit_latency_ms; floor 1.5 x pre_prepare_latency_ms+prepare_latency_ms+commit_latency_ms + 100 ms; range 100-3000 ms.

Phases: p0 base, p1 base + hold D300, p2 5+50, p3 5+50 + straggler 30 + hold D550, p4 5+50 + straggler 300 + hold D350, p5 5+150 + straggler 30, p6 5+150 + straggler 450, p7 base (p3 and p5 straggler 60 until 2026-10-22).

Rows under restart-v4 (2026-10-22), recalibrated on restart-cal9 with the receipt-dated timer and a 30 ms straggler in p3 and p5 (60 ms until 2026-10-22, under which 650/150 lost p3 to 650/10). The SBFT simulator ranks p5 only on a tie (DESIGN.md section 8).

Rows ran under frozen code restart-v4 (a424745337c6), restart-v7 (30dcd0ed8351).

## Tendermint

N 5,000; ladder 100/150/400, opening 150; heuristic k 0.53 on p50_consensus_latency_ms; floor 0.33 x p50_consensus_latency_ms; range 100-3000 ms.

Phases: p0 base, p1 base + slow proposer, p2 5+50, p3 5+50 + slow proposer, p4 5+150, p5 base.

Oracle and discrete arms changed (Kevin, 2026-10-23): the oracle is 150 ms in p2 and p3, the best measured p3 on prefix cells of the compressed spec (125 +0.17 with stalls, 150 +0.54, 175 +0.53; 200 +0.51 on the full spec), and the discrete arms are 100/150/400 from restart-v5 (100/200/400 before; those discrete rows and the 200 ms oracle draws were removed). The oracle row ran under restart-v4 from the same schedule (a file outside the code hash). The heuristic and the continuous rows opened at 200 ms, the earlier middle rung; the opening covers only the first decision, in p0, where every timeout ties. Second draw (restart-v7, Kevin 2026-10-25: kept and averaged with the first): every row opened at 150 ms, and the pretrained continuous GP and PPO rows loaded the restart-v5 priors, which differ from the restart-v2 priors their first draw loaded (the opening is the simulator's starting action); the LinUCB priors are identical.

Rows ran under frozen code restart-v2 (570591a4480e), restart-v4 (a424745337c6), restart-v5 (7e5668437da4), restart-v7 (30dcd0ed8351).

## PBFT studies

PBFT only (runs/2026-10-23-pbft-studies/spec.md; draws 2 and 3: runs/2026-10-27-study-draws/spec.md). Each study changes one thing against E0, the PBFT table above (same engine, priors array-identical), and runs every variant where it applies. Scored as above; a configuration's values are the mean over its draws (E0's too), and each "vs" is the configuration's value (mean R, throughput, latency) minus E0's for the same configuration, e.g. E1 discrete_gp pretrained minus the PBFT table's discrete_gp pretrained, not the oracle (E3 and E8: minus their own oracle, since their specs differ). Throughput and latency are the means over the phases. The summaries give each draw's value, and the per-phase tables every draw as its own row. Calibration cells are in each study's calibration/ folder.

### E3 too few rungs

Changes: the E3 spec (results/studies/pbft/sparse/failure_spec_e3_r1.xml: p0 base, p1 base + D300, p2 5+50 + D150, p3 5+50 + D500, p4 5+150 + D350, p5 5+150, p6 base), whose phases need five timeouts (250/350/550/800/1200); the agent has 250/550/1200, so p2 (350) and p4 (800) are off its ladder. Compared with the 3-rung oracle, the best the agent's ladder allows.

### E7 data poisoning

Changes: replica 0 (the first f) shares random reports in every phase (results/studies/pbft/poisoning/failure_spec_e7.xml, the main spec plus <agent><dirtyReport>): each feature of its shared report is its own value scaled by a random 0.4 to 2.0 (2.2 for the p90/p95 percentiles), its counts drawn from 0 to 3 times their value; the reward it shares is untouched. The oracle reads no report, so the comparison is each row against E0's same row.

### E8 non-uniform network

Changes: a static extra one-way delay per replica pair on top of every phase (results/studies/pbft/nonuniform/failure_spec_e8.xml): the replicas sit on a line at 0, 0, 10 and 30 ms, a pair's extra is the distance between them, so one-way delays are 5 to 35 ms at base, 55 to 85 at 5+50 and 155 to 185 at 5+150. The rows run in sharing mode and, marked "no sharing", in learning mode (each replica from its own report, as E4). Compared with E8's own oracle (the main oracle schedule on this spec), since the spec differs.

## PBFT scalability

PBFT only (runs/2026-10-28-pbft-scalability/spec.md). The real spec and the main oracle schedule at N replicas, two per machine on N/2 machines plus the client's machine (one draw = one run of 2,110 s), on the scalability draft of the engine (changes 1-8 and 10 of the spec: measured recovery budget, striped client, NewView by digests, one held leader per tick, single-source fetches, and the 32 MiB channel warm-up at start that pre-grows gRPC 1.51's flow-control windows, without which a never-led leader's first 4.2 MB pre-prepare took 6-8 round trips at 5+50 and the oracle's p4 stormed at every size above 4), the pretrained rows loading the n=4 priors unchanged, the rate 20,000 throughout. The draft is not frozen: each row.json records the run's code hash. Scored as above; "vs" is the configuration's value minus E0's, the PBFT table's row of the same configuration at four replicas on the frozen engine (mean over draws on both sides). Rows are the oracle, the heuristic, and the best pretrained and untrained model of each action space, floored.
